### BoK Gate check (v3 — MCORCH Master Execution Protocol)

For each BoK suite in `docs/bok/<slug>/`, verify completeness:
```bash
for slug in $(ls docs/bok/ 2>/dev/null); do
  required=(00-index 01-mrd 02-brd 03-prd 04-frd 05-sdd 06-data-model 07-process-flow 08-quality-metrics)
  missing=()
  for doc in "${required[@]}"; do
    [ -f "docs/bok/$slug/$doc.md" ] || missing+=("$doc")
  done
  [ ${#missing[@]} -eq 0 ] && echo "✅ $slug — BoK complete" || echo "⚠️ $slug — missing: ${missing[*]}"
done
```

If the user mentions working on a **new module / complex feature / integration / architectural change**:
- Check whether `docs/bok/<expected-slug>/` exists with all 9 docs.
- If missing: surface in the **ALERTAS** section as a critical block per CLAUDE.md MCORCH Master Execution Protocol.
- Recommend `/bok-scribe <idea summary>` before any code is written.

## Rules

- **TypeScript errors → report immediately.** If there are TS errors, list them under ALERTAS. Do not start implementing new features until errors are resolved (unless the user explicitly asks to proceed despite errors).
- **Unhealthy containers → report with triage.** Suggest the fix command if known (e.g., `docker compose restart mcorch-vector-engine` for chroma).
- **Uncommitted changes → describe, do not commit.** Only describe what exists in the working tree; committing is the user's call.
- **Keep the brief tight.** No padding. No repetition of CLAUDE.md architecture. The user already knows the architecture — they need the delta: what changed, what's broken, what's next.
- **Pending Actions priority order:** CRITICAL (before first client) > NON-CRITICAL > Phase Zeta > Core v3 > Epsilon v9.
- **Knowledge Mesh numbers:** use `GraphRAG State` section from HANDOFF.md as the source. Do not invent counts.

> 7607	### Embedding Model
> 7608	`openai/text-embedding-3-small`, `dimensions: 768` (truncated output via OpenRouter).
> 7609	Schema: `vector(768)` — HNSW cosine index.
> 7610	
> 7611	### Embedding Pipeline (Autônomo — 2026-05-03)
> 7612	Três camadas eliminam intervenção manual:
> 7613	1. **INSERT trigger** — `trg_mcorch_embed_on_insert` (pg_net) dispara `embed-mcorch-node` assincronamente em todo INSERT onde `embedding IS NULL`. Nunca bloqueia o INSERT.
> 7614	2. **Post-commit hook** — `.git/hooks/post-commit` roda `node scripts/ingest-codebase.ts --quiet` em background após cada commit → re-ingesta AST.
> 7615	3. **MeshControlPanel** — UI bottom-left do MatrixPage: "Sync Embeddings" (`trigger-embeddings`) e "Refresh Mesh Stats" (`trigger-ingest`).
> 7616	
> 7617	### Comandos de manutenção da Mesh
> 7618	```bash
> 7619	# Verificar nós sem embedding (deveria retornar array vazio):
> 7620	curl -s "https://bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa.supabase.co/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?embedding=is.null&select=id,name" \
> 7621	  -H "apikey: $SB_SECRET_KEY" | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(f'{len(d)} nós sem embedding')"
> 7622	
> 7623	# Backfill manual (Bun-native — requer .env):
> 7624	bun run scripts/backfill-embeddings.ts
> 7625	
> 7626	# Backfill de código-fonte nos nós AST:
> 7627	bun run scripts/backfill-node-content.ts
> 7628	
> 7629	# Re-sync AST completo:
> 7630	cd packages/gitnexus-core && npm run sync-mesh
> 7631	
> 7632	# Acompanhar re-ingesta pós-commit:
> 7633	tail -f /tmp/mcorch-ingest.log
> 7634	```

> 7638	## Video Rescue Protocol
> 7639	
> 7640	When a video generation operation (Google Veo) times out or client disconnects:
> 7641	```bash
> 7642	# Via edge function (production):
> 7643	curl -X POST "https://bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa.supabase.co/functions/v1/rescue-video" \
> 7644	  -H "Authorization: Bearer <user_jwt>" \
> 7645	  -d '{"content_id":"<uuid>","operation_id":"<operation_name>"}'
> 7646	
> 7647	# Via local script (admin/debug):
> 7648	source .env && bun run rescue.ts <operation_id> <content_id>
> 7649	```

> 7741	## Constellation Matrix Intelligence & Stabilization Record (2026-05-03)
> 7742	
> 7743	Sessão de elevação da `ConstellationMatrix` ao nível de **Code Intelligence** e estabilização do motor gráfico. O motor Sigma.js/ForceAtlas2 apresentava loops de renderização infinitos ("blinking") e resets de estado ao interagir com a sidebar. A correção envolveu a estabilização de referências de props, memoização rigorosa das opções do grafo e desacoplamento de filtros dos ciclos de renderização do canvas. Paralelamente, a Matrix foi dotada de inteligência de código nativa via hook `useNodeIntelligence` e um `NodeDetailsPanel` reestruturado com navegação por abas.

> 7746	|--------|--------|
> 7747	| `src/hooks/useNodeIntelligence.ts` — criado | ✅ Orquestração de busca de código (`mcorch_nodes`) + análise semântica simulada Gemini |
> 7748	| `src/components/orchestrator/NodeDetailsPanel.tsx` | ✅ Navegação por abas (Meta, Code, Brain) + Code Preview + IA Analysis flow |
> 7749	| `src/components/orchestrator/ConstellationMatrix.tsx` | ✅ Estabilização Sigma.js: memoized options + stable refs + engine integration fix |
> 7750	| `src/hooks/useConstellationSigma.ts` | ✅ `useRef` para event handlers + callbacks estáveis — fim do "blinking" |
> 7751	| `src/components/ui/progress.tsx` | ✅ Prop `indicatorClassName` adicionado para estilização dinâmica da barra de IA |

> 1	// vision-mcp-core auth/identity.ts — FR-VM-002 (ES256 JWKS fail-closed) + FR-VM-001 (Origin gate).
> 2	// SOP: docs/processes/[[edge-jwt-identity-verification|edge-jwt-identity-verification]].md. NUNCA atob cego no sub (anti-confused-deputy).
> 3	// This is the gate the gitnexus MCP precedent did NOT have (it was internal/unauthenticated) — the
> 4	// SDD §5.3 mandate for the first tenant-facing service outside the Supabase gateway.
> 5	
> 6	import { createRemoteJWKSet, jwtVerify } from "jose";
> 7	
> 8	const SUPABASE_URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL ?? "";
> 9	
> 10	// createRemoteJWKSet caches keys in-process and refetches on unknown kid; we build it lazily so the
> 11	// module imports cleanly in tests where SUPABASE_URL may be unset.
> 12	let _jwks: ReturnType<typeof createRemoteJWKSet> | null = null;
> 13	function jwks() {
> 14	  if (!SUPABASE_URL) throw new Error("SUPABASE_URL not configured");
> 15	  if (!_jwks) {
> 16	    _jwks = createRemoteJWKSet(new URL(`${SUPABASE_URL}/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`));
> 17	  }
> 18	  return _jwks;
> 19	}
> 20	
> 21	export interface Identity {
> 22	  sub: string; // verified user id — the ONLY source of identity for any downstream effect
> 23	}
> 24	
> 25	export type IdentityResult =
> 26	  | { ok: true; identity: Identity }
> 27	  | { ok: false; status: 401; code: string };
> 28	
> 29	const PRM_URL_PATH = "/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource";
> 30	
> 31	// RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata pointer for the 401 WWW-Authenticate challenge.
> 32	export function wwwAuthenticate(publicBase: string): string {
> 33	  return `Bearer resource_metadata="${publicBase.replace(/\/$/, "")}${PRM_URL_PATH}"`;
> 34	}
> 35	
> 36	// FR-VM-002: verify the bearer JWT via ES256 JWKS BEFORE trusting `sub`. Fail-closed on ANY error
> 37	// (missing/invalid/expired token, unreachable JWKS, wrong alg). Never decodes the payload blindly.
> 38	export async function verifyBearer(authHeader: string | undefined): Promise<IdentityResult> {
> 39	  if (!authHeader || !authHeader.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
> 40	    return { ok: false, status: 401, code: "identity_unverified" };
> 41	  }
> 42	  const token = authHeader.slice("Bearer ".length).trim();
> 43	  if (!token) return { ok: false, status: 401, code: "identity_unverified" };
> 44	  try {
> 45	    const { payload } = await jwtVerify(token, jwks(), { algorithms: ["ES256"] });
> 46	    const sub = typeof payload.sub === "string" ? payload.sub : "";
> 47	    if (!sub) return { ok: false, status: 401, code: "identity_unverified" };
> 48	    return { ok: true, identity: { sub } };
> 49	  } catch {
> 50	    // fail-closed: JWKS unreachable, bad signature, expired, alg mismatch — never process.
> 51	    return { ok: false, status: 401, code: "identity_unverified" };
> 52	  }
> 53	}
> 54	
> 55	// FR-VM-001: Origin allowlist (anti DNS-rebinding). Requests with no Origin (server-to-server,
> 56	// e.g. MCP clients / curl) are allowed; a PRESENT Origin must be in the allowlist.
> 57	export function isOriginAllowed(origin: string | undefined, allowlist: string[]): boolean {
> 58	  if (!origin) return true; // non-browser clients omit Origin
> 59	  return allowlist.includes(origin);
> 60	}
> 61	
> 62	export const PROTOCOL_VERSION = "2025-11-25";
> 63	
> 64	export function isProtocolVersionOk(header: string | undefined): boolean {
> 65	  // Absent header tolerated for backward-compat clients; if present it must match the pinned rev.
> 66	  if (!header) return true;
> 67	  return header === PROTOCOL_VERSION;
> 68	}

> 26	export interface MeshSearchResult {

> 28	  cost_mco: number; // Fatia 1 keyword scan = 0 (no provider leg); pgvector variant = 1 (Fatia 2)
> 29	  scope: string;
> 30	}
> 31	
> 32	// Builds the tenant-scoped PostgREST URL. EXPORTED for unit testing the scoping invariant without a
> 33	// live DB — the test asserts the user_id filter is always present (no cross-tenant query is possible).
> 34	export function buildSearchUrl(sub: string, query: string, topK: number): string {
> 35	  const q = encodeURIComponent(`%${query}%`);
> 36	  // or=(user_id.eq.<sub>,user_id.is.null) → tenant's own nodes + system nodes only. NEVER unscoped.
> 37	  const scope = `or=(user_id.eq.${sub},user_id.is.null)`;
> 38	  const filter = `or=(name.ilike.${q},content.ilike.${q})`;
> 39	  return (
> 40	    `${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes` +
> 41	    `?select=id,name,node_type,content&${scope}&${filter}&limit=${topK}`
> 42	  );
> 43	}
> 44	
> 45	export async function meshSearch(input: MeshSearchInput, sub: string): Promise<MeshSearchResult> {
> 46	  const topK = Math.min(Math.max(input.top_k ?? 10, 1), 50);
> 47	  const url = buildSearchUrl(sub, input.query, topK);
> 48	  const res = await fetch(url, {
> 49	    headers: { apikey: SB_SECRET_KEY, Authorization: `Bearer ${SB_SECRET_KEY}` },
> 50	  });
> 51	  if (!res.ok) {
> 52	    throw new Error(`mesh_search_failed:${res.status}`);
> 53	  }
> 54	  const rows = (await res.json()) as Array<{ id: string; name: string; node_type: string; content: string | null }>;
> 55	  return {
> 56	    matches: rows.map((r) => ({
> 57	      node_id: r.id,
> 58	      name: r.name,
> 59	      node_type: r.node_type,
> 60	      snippet: (r.content ?? "").slice(0, 200),
> 61	    })),
> 62	    cost_mco: 0,
> 63	    scope: `user:${sub}+system`,
> 64	  };
> 65	}
> 66	
> ```

> 1	// vision-mcp-core safety/sentinel.ts — VERBATIM port of supabase/functions/_shared/sentinel.ts
> 2	// inspection path (FR-VM-005). Anti-drift: the L1/L2/L3 logic is identical to the edge sentinel
> 3	// (the security BoK SSOT). Only the Deno-specific Response/telemetry helpers are dropped here —
> 4	// the container logs via infra/telemetry.ts instead.
> 5	
> 6	export interface SentinelVerdict {
> 7	  allow: boolean;
> 8	  status: number;
> 9	  code: string;
> 10	  detail?: string;
> 11	}
> 12	
> 13	const MAX_PROMPT_BYTES = 64_000;
> 14	
> 15	// Independent injection pattern families. Score >= 2 (two distinct families) blocks.
> 16	const INJECTION_PATTERNS: RegExp[] = [
> 17	  /ignore\s+(?:(?:all|any|the|every|previous|prior|above|earlier|preceding)\s+){1,3}(instructions?|rules?|prompts?|directions?|commands?|context|guidelines?)/i,
> 18	  /disregard\s+(the\s+)?(above|previous|prior|system)/i,
> 19	  /reveal\s+(your\s+)?(system\s+prompt|hidden\s+instructions|secrets?)/i,
> 20	  /you\s+are\s+now\s+(in\s+)?(developer|dan|jailbreak|god)\s*mode/i,
> 21	  /act\s+as\s+(if\s+you\s+were\s+)?(root|admin|system|the\s+operator)/i,
> 22	  /\bBEGIN\s+(SYSTEM|ADMIN|OVERRIDE)\b/,
> 23	  /<\s*\/?\s*(system|assistant|tool)\s*>/i,
> 24	  /\[[0-9;]*[A-Za-z]/, // ANSI CSI escape smuggled into a prompt
> 25	  /[A-Za-z0-9+/]{400,}={0,2}/, // anomalous base64 blob
> 26	  /\bdo\s+not\s+(charge|bill|debit|deduct)\b/i, // billing-bypass coaching
> 27	];
> 28	
> 29	// eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex
> 30	const ANSI_OR_CONTROL_RE = /[\x00-\x08\x0b\x0c\x0e-\x1f\x7f-\x9f]/;
> 31	
> 32	export function lexicalScore(text: string): number {
> 33	  let score = 0;
> 34	  for (const re of INJECTION_PATTERNS) {
> 35	    if (re.test(text)) score += 1;
> 36	  }
> 37	  return score;
> 38	}
> 39	
> 40	const BUCKETS = new Map<string, { tokens: number; refillAt: number }>();
> 41	const RATE = { capacity: 30, refillMs: 60_000 };
> 42	
> 43	export function takeToken(identity: string, now: number = Date.now()): boolean {
> 44	  const bucket = BUCKETS.get(identity) ?? { tokens: RATE.capacity, refillAt: now + RATE.refillMs };
> 45	  if (now >= bucket.refillAt) {
> 46	    bucket.tokens = RATE.capacity;
> 47	    bucket.refillAt = now + RATE.refillMs;
> 48	  }
> 49	  if (bucket.tokens <= 0) {
> 50	    BUCKETS.set(identity, bucket);
> 51	    return false;
> 52	  }
> 53	  bucket.tokens -= 1;
> 54	  BUCKETS.set(identity, bucket);
> 55	  return true;
> 56	}
> 57	
> 58	export interface InspectOptions {
> 59	  rateLimit?: boolean;
> 60	}
> 61	
> 62	// FR-VM-005a: inbound args inspection. Also reused (FR-VM-005b) over extracted text before any
> 63	// mesh insert — but Fatia 1 only exposes mesh.search (read-only), so the inbound path is what ships.
> 64	export function inspectPrompt(raw: string, identity: string, opts: InspectOptions = {}): SentinelVerdict {
> 65	  const { rateLimit = true } = opts;
> 66	  if (typeof raw !== "string" || raw.length === 0) {
> 67	    return { allow: false, status: 400, code: "empty_prompt" };
> 68	  }
> 69	  if (raw.length > MAX_PROMPT_BYTES) {
> 70	    return { allow: false, status: 413, code: "payload_too_large" };
> 71	  }
> 72	  if (rateLimit && !takeToken(identity)) {
> 73	    return { allow: false, status: 429, code: "rate_limited" };
> 74	  }
> 75	  if (ANSI_OR_CONTROL_RE.test(raw)) {
> 76	    return { allow: false, status: 403, code: "control_chars_in_prompt" };
> 77	  }
> 78	  const score = lexicalScore(raw);
> 79	  if (score >= 2) {
> 80	    return { allow: false, status: 403, code: "prompt_injection_suspected", detail: `score=${score}` };
> 81	  }
> 82	  return { allow: true, status: 200, code: "ok" };
> 83	}
> 84	
> ```

> #!/usr/bin/env bun
> /**
>  * Mint a REAL user access_token (JWT) for authenticated edge-fn calls in QA — without a browser or raw password.
>  * Admin generateLink (magiclink) → verifyOtp(token_hash) → session.access_token. Per-user, reproducible.
>  * Pattern extends gen-magiclink.ts (v6.20.4). The token is one-time and ~1h-lived; mint right before use.
>  *
>  * Usage: bun run scripts/qa/gen-user-jwt.ts <email>
>  *   → prints the access_token on stdout (nothing else), or an error on stderr + exit 1.
>  *   e.g. TOKEN=$(bun run scripts/qa/gen-user-jwt.ts gabrielcall@gmail.com)
>  */
> import { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js';
> 
> const URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL!;
> const SVC = (process.env.SB_SECRET_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY)!;
> const ANON = (process.env.SB_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_ANON_KEY)!;
> const email = process.argv[2];
> if (!URL || !SVC || !ANON || !email) {
>   console.error('Usage: gen-user-jwt.ts <email>  (needs SUPABASE_URL + SB_SECRET_KEY + SB_PUBLISHABLE_KEY)');
>   process.exit(1);
> }
> 
> const admin = createClient(URL, SVC, { auth: { persistSession: false } });
> const anon = createClient(URL, ANON, { auth: { persistSession: false } });
> 
> const { data, error } = await admin.auth.admin.generateLink({ type: 'magiclink', email });
> if (error || !data.properties?.hashed_token) {
>   console.error('generateLink error:', error?.message ?? 'no hashed_token');
>   process.exit(1);
> }
> const tokenHash = data.properties.hashed_token;
> 
> let accessToken: string | null = null;
> for (const type of ['magiclink', 'email'] as const) {
>   const { data: sess, error: vErr } = await anon.auth.verifyOtp({ token_hash: tokenHash, type });
>   if (!vErr && sess?.session?.access_token) { accessToken = sess.session.access_token; break; }
> }
> if (!accessToken) { console.error('verifyOtp failed for both magiclink/email types'); process.exit(1); }
> console.log(accessToken);

## 🤖 Claude *(16:24:17)*

All clear:
- `_protocolVersion` is unset until negotiation → the **initialize** request sends *no* protocol header (server tolerates absent); post-initialize it's `2025-11-25` (both client & server are SDK 1.29.0, LATEST) → the strict gate accepts. No 400 risk.
- `requestInit.headers` is merged into every request → I can inject `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>`; Node fetch sends no `Origin` → Origin gate passes.
- Server transport doesn't pre-empt the custom gate.

The harness design (the real deliverable is **cross-tenant isolation**, Owner's concern): create two throwaway tenants A & B, seed an A-owned node, a B-owned node, and a system node sharing one ILIKE marker; mint a real ES256 JWT per tenant; drive the full MCP handshake via the SDK client; assert A sees `{A-node, system}` but **not** B-node, and B sees `{B-node, system}` but **not** A-node. That proves the verified `sub` from the JWT actually drives the filter — not a spoofable constant. Plus: sentinel fires on a hostile tool arg, and no-token → 401 within the same harness.

Let me write it.

G0 (health) and G1 (no-token→401) pass. But the tenant-A handshake dies: `initialize` succeeds, then the SDK's follow-up `notifications/initialized` POST gets **HTTP 500** from the server. This is the server's `transport.handleRequest(...).catch(() => 500)` firing. This smells like a real defect in how the server uses a *single shared* `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` with `sessionIdGenerator: undefined` — which would mean **no real MCP client can complete a handshake**. Let me confirm against the SDK's server transport logic and the container logs.

## 🤖 Claude *(16:26:54)*

The single shared `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` reused across requests is the suspect — the SDK's documented stateless pattern is **fresh transport+server per request**, and the deploy's curl gates only exercised 401/403/400 (never a full initialize→notification→call session), so this defect slipped through. Before choosing the fix (stateless-per-request vs. stateful session map), I need to confirm how the SDK's web-standard transport handles the "must initialize first" rule in stateless mode — that determines whether per-request fresh transports interoperate with the standard client's `initialize → initialized → call` sequence.

## 🤖 Claude *(16:27:17)*


## 🤖 Claude *(16:27:19)*

Line 137-139 is the smoking gun — the SDK **explicitly** forbids reusing a stateless transport across requests (`_hasHandledRequest` guard), which is exactly our 500 on the 2nd POST. The server creates one transport at startup and reuses it. Confirmed server defect: **no real MCP client can complete a handshake.** Let me read the exact stateless-vs-stateful validation branches to pick the correct fix.

## 🤖 Claude *(16:28:39)*


## 🤖 Claude *(16:28:42)*

Decisive and confirmed. In **stateless mode** (`sessionIdGenerator === undefined`), `validateSession` returns `undefined` immediately (line 585-589) — so a *fresh* transport handles `notifications/initialized` and `tools/call` without needing a prior initialize in that instance. But the SDK **forbids reusing** a stateless transport (line 137-141 `_hasHandledRequest` → throws). The server reuses one transport from startup → 2nd POST throws → our `.catch` → 500. 

**This is a real Fatia 1 defect the E2E just caught: no real MCP client could ever complete a handshake** — the deploy's curl gates only tested 401/403/400, never a full session. The fix matches the SDK's own mandate: fresh `McpServer`+transport per request. Let me apply it and read the Dockerfile to deploy correctly.

- **G4**: tenant A → `matches=2` (own node `42ddf117` + system `39f66f09`), **leakedB=false**

The E2E caught a real Fatia 1 defect (shared stateless transport → no MCP client could handshake) — exactly the deploy-material value the handoff flagged. Now, per the Owner's blast-radius concern (cross-tenant leak) and ultracode, I won't declare victory on a happy-path green: I'll adversarially verify it. First a reusable call-driver, then the two security-crux probes myself, then a multi-lens refutation workflow.

null : arg(\"token\") ?? process.env.VISION_MCP_JWT ?? null;\nconst query = arg(\"query\") ?? argv.find((a) => !a.startsWith(\"--\")) ?? \"\";\nconst topK = arg(\"top_k\") ? parseInt(arg(\"top_k\")!, 10) : 50;\n\nif (!query && !noToken) {\n  console.error('Usage: vision-mcp-call.ts --token \"<jwt>\" --query \"<q>\" [--top_k 50]  (or --no-token)');\n  process.exit(3);\n}\n\nasync function main() {\n  const headers: Record<string, string> = {};\n  if (token) headers[\"Authorization\"] = `Bearer ${token}`;\n  const transport = new StreamableHTTPClientTransport(new URL(MCP_URL), { requestInit: { headers } });\n  const client = new Client({ name: \"vision-mcp-call\", version: \"1.0.0\" }, { capabilities: {} });\n  try {\n    await client.connect(transport);\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.log(JSON.stringify({ ok: false, connectError: String(e).slice(0, 300) }));\n    process.exit(0);\n  }\n  try {\n    const res: any = await client.callTool({ name: \"mesh_search\", arguments: { query, top_k: topK } });\n    const text = res.content?.[0]?.type === \"text\" ? ## 🤖 Claude *(16:34:09)*

**6/6 adversarial probes pass** — the two security-crux properties hold materially:
- **A2/A3/A4**: tampered-payload, `alg:none`, and HS256-forged tokens all → `401 identity_unverified`. The server genuinely **ES256-verifies against the JWKS** — it does not decode `sub` blindly (anti-confused-deputy mandate confirmed).
- **B1**: 7 PostgREST-breakout payloads (`)&user_id=eq.<B>`, `,user_id.is.not.null`, `OR true --`, `select=*&user_id=neq.<A>`, `*`, `%`, …) — **zero** leak B's node. `encodeURIComponent` neutralizes the operators; scope filter is always ANDed (the `*`/`%` wildcards return 50 rows but all A-owned+system, never B).

Now the ultracode adversarial-review workflow: independent lenses to scrutinize the **server fix itself** (correctness/regressions/concurrency), hunt auth-bypass and isolation holes beyond mine, and act as a completeness critic on what the gap actually demanded. Let me author and run it.

## 🤖 Claude *(16:35:43)*

O workflow adversarial (4 lentes em paralelo) está rodando em background — me notifica ao concluir. Resumo material até aqui, Maestro:

## Estado — mesh.search E2E (Fatia 1)

**Bug real encontrado e corrigido** (valor do deploy material, exatamente o padrão que o handoff sinalizou): o servidor criava **um único** `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` no startup e o reusava. O SDK MCP 1.29.0 **proíbe** reuso de transport stateless (guard `_hasHandledRequest`) → `initialize` dava 200 mas `notifications/initialized` dava **500**. Ou seja: **nenhum cliente MCP real conseguia completar o handshake** — os gates curl do deploy só testaram 401/403/400, nunca uma sessão completa. Corrigido para `McpServer`+transport **frescos por request** (mandato do próprio SDK em modo stateless). Imagem rebuildada, container recriado, **artefato servido verificado** (Lei 1).

| Prova | Resultado |
|---|---|
| E2E handshake completo (`scripts/qa/e2e-vision-mcp-mesh-search.ts`) | ✅ **7/7** — health · no-token→401 · initialize c/ JWT ES256 real · tools/list=[mesh_search] · **isolamento cross-tenant** (A vê próprio+sistema, NÃO o de B; B espelhado) · sentinel bloqueia injeção in-MCP |
| Adversarial (`scripts/qa/probe-vision-mcp-adversarial.ts`) | ✅ **6/6** — token forjado rejeitado 3 formas (payload adulterado · `alg:none` · HS256) provando verificação ES256 **real** (não `atob` cego) · 7 payloads de injeção PostgREST no `query`, **zero leak** de B |
| tsc (root + package) · unit suite | ✅ 0 erros · 14/14 |

A última ponta não-provada da Fatia 1 está **fechada com prova material** — e o E2E + os probes ficam como regressão permanente (anticorpo do bug de transport).

Aguardando o workflow adversarial (lentes: correção-do-fix/leak/concorrência · bypass-de-auth · isolamento-profundo · crítico-de-completude) para escrutínio independente antes de declarar vitória e selar.

header?: string }> = [\n  { label: \"2025-11-25 (latest/pinned)\", header: \"2025-11-25\" },\n  { label: \"2025-06-18 (current, widely deployed)\", header: \"2025-06-18\" },\n  { label: \"2025-03-26 (SDK DEFAULT negotiated)\", header: \"2025-03-26\" },\n  { label: \"2024-11-05 (legacy supported)\", header: \"2024-11-05\" },\n  { label: \"(absent header)\", header: undefined },\n  { label: \"1999-01-01 (bogus \u2014 must 400)\", header: \"1999-01-01\" },\n];\n\nasync function main() {\n  const run = `protover${Date.now().toString(36)}`;\n  const email = `qa+${run}@vm.local`;\n  const { data: u, error: ue } = await admin.auth.admin.createUser({ email, email_confirm: true });\n  if (ue || !u.user) throw new Error(`createUser: ${ue?.message}`);\n  const { data: l } = await admin.auth.admin.generateLink({ type: \"magiclink\", email });\n  let jwt = \"\";\n  for (const t of [\"magiclink\", \"email\"] as const) {\n    const { data: s, error } = await anon.auth.verifyOtp({ token_hash: l!.properties!.hashed_token, type: t });\n    if (!error && s?.session) { jwt = s.session.access_token; A PRESENT\n  // header must be a version the MCP SDK ITSELF supports \u2014 NOT only the latest literal. A client that\n  // negotiates an older-but-supported rev (the SDK default negotiated version is 2025-03-26; 2025-06-18\n  // is widely deployed) completes `initialize` and then sends `MCP-Protocol-Version: <negotiated>` on\n  // every follow-up; pinning to a single version 400s those legitimate clients (FR-VM-001 interop).\n  if (!header) return true;\n  return (SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS as readonly string[]).includes(header);\n}"
}

## 🤖 Claude *(16:53:18)*

Tudo concluído e materialmente provado, Maestro. Resumo executivo:

## ✅ Passo 1 fechado — `mesh.search` E2E com JWT (Fatia 1)

A última ponta não-provada está fechada — e o E2E **pagou dividendos**: pegou **2 defeitos reais** que os gates curl do deploy e os unit tests jamais veriam.

### Defeitos encontrados e corrigidos (com prova before/after)

**① Handshake MCP quebrado (crítico) — `src/mcp/server.ts`**
O servidor reusava **um único** `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` criado no startup. O SDK MCP 1.29.0 **proíbe** reuso de transport stateless (guard `_hasHandledRequest`) → `initialize` dava 200 mas `notifications/initialized` dava **500**. Tradução: **nenhum cliente MCP real conseguia completar o handshake**. Os gates do deploy só testaram 401/403/400, nunca uma sessão completa. Corrigido para `McpServer`+transport **frescos por request** (mandato do próprio SDK).

**② Gate de protocolo estrito demais (alto — interop) — `src/auth/identity.ts`**
`isProtocolVersionOk` aceitava **só** `2025-11-25`. Um cliente 3rd-party que negocia `2025-06-18`/`2025-03-26` (ambos suportados pelo SDK; `2025-03-26` é o default) completa `initialize` e leva **400** em todo follow-up. Meu E2E não pegou (o cliente do SDK negocia `2025-11-25`). Corrigido para aceitar qualquer `SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS`. Achado pela **lente crítico-de-completude** do workflow adversarial.

### Prova material (contra o container servido — Lei 1)

| Verificação | Antes | Depois |
|---|---|---|
| **E2E handshake + isolamento cross-tenant + sentinel** | falhava no 500 | **7/7** ✅ |
| **Adversarial** (forja ES256 ×3 + 7 payloads de injeção PostgREST) | — | **6/6** ✅ |
| **Interop de protocolo** (probe dedicado) | 3 revs suportadas → **400** | todas → **200**, bogus → 400 ✅ |
| **Suíte unit** (pacote) | 14/14 | **15/15** ✅ (+regressão cross-version) |
| **Telemetria** `infra_health_logs service='vision-mcp'` | — | rows healthy 16:50:19 ✅ |
| **Workflow adversarial** (4 lentes, 359k tokens) | — | fix-correctness ✅ · isolamento ✅ · auth-bypass LOW · completude→1 must-fix (corrigido) |

**Isolamento (o crux do Owner)** confirmado por **dois métodos independentes**: A vê `{próprio, sistema}` e nunca o nó de B; o `or=(scope) AND or=(filter)` é load-bearing (um `or` colapsado *vazaria* — provado). Forja de token rejeitada 3 formas (payload adulterado · `alg:none` · HS256) ⇒ verificação ES256 **real**, não `atob` cego.

### Síntese / anticorpos (Obstacle→Synthesis, antes de qualquer seal)
- **4 scripts QA permanentes** (regressão dos 2 bugs): `e2e-vision-mcp-mesh-search.ts` · `probe-vision-mcp-adversarial.ts` · `probe-vision-mcp-protocol-versions.ts` · `vision-mcp-call.ts` (driver reutilizável).
- **FRD `04-frd.md`** alinhado ao contrato real de `MCP-Protocol-Version`.
- **Risco residual orphan-sub** (LOW — token de user deletado vale ≤1h, limitado a próprio+sistema) documentado no código; a registrar como OTD na emenda BoK da Fatia 2.

### Resíduos honestos (não-bloqueantes)
- `vhost nginx mcp.mcorch.com` (OTD-VM-013) segue pendente — ação Sovereign (root/DNS).
- `mesh.search` é keyword ILIKE; variante pgvector é Fatia 2 (documentado, `cost_mco=0`).

**Survival Laws:** Lei 1 ✓ (toda claim ancorada em output verificado contra o container; before/after dos 2 fixes) · Lei 2 ✓ (FRD alinhado, risco residual documentado) · Lei 3 ✓ (workflow retornou síntese, não dumps) · Lei 4 ✓ (ORO declarado; Owner=Sovereign).

O working tree tem as mudanças (3 fontes + FRD + 4 scripts QA) **não-commitadas** — deixei para você revisar. Recomendo selar agora via **`/handoff`** (um defeito real num serviço tenant-facing foi corrigido e deployado — ponto natural de seal), ou, se preferir aproveitar a janela, posso puxar o próximo item da Fatia 1 (ex.: smoke de cliente heterogêneo real / `mcp-remote`) ou começar a **Fatia 2**. Como prefere seguir?
